#### 3.1.1 Post-ledger author-self-review allocation supplement

Each row below preserves these explicit fields unless the row says otherwise: owner/source `JAN-CSAA-000@0.3.0 / §17`; applicability/subject this exact authored controlled-document Draft after objective closure; disposition `ALLOCATED_TO_POST_LEDGER_SELF_REVIEW`; implementation and verification state `NOT_REQUIRED_CURRENT_PHASE`; verification binding `JAN-CSAA-005-VER-SELF-001`; preliminary SELF performed/nonpass with two MAJOR findings; corrective SELF-REVIEW-002 activated but unperformed/nonpass and unable to complete before exact final refresh; evidence preliminary SELF-REVIEW-001 plus future exact corrective review; no satisfaction or pass inferred.

| Requirement ID | Exact inherited obligation |
| --- | --- |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-668` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 1: does the document convert derived evidence into authority? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-669` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 2: does it claim completeness where reflection, dynamic loading, unsupported syntax, partial indexing, or missing traces make completeness impossible? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-670` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 3: can stale or mixed-revision facts appear current? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-671` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 4: can a tool fail, time out, or skip files and still produce a green result? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-672` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 5: can two providers disagree without the disagreement remaining visible? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-673` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 6: can normalization erase provider provenance or limitations? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-674` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 7: can an exception be created by configuration silence rather than approved record? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-675` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 8: can the fixture oracle be generated by the same code it judges? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-676` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 9: can the conformance suite pass with an empty graph, empty rule registry, or analyzer that returns no Analyzer Finding Records? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-677` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 10: can an incremental index diverge from a full rebuild without detection? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-678` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 11: can generated code, declaration files, tests, examples, or vendor code be misclassified? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-679` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 12: can a runtime trace from another build or environment be attached to the subject? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-680` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 13: can “no static callers” be reported as proven dead code despite unresolved dynamic entry? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-681` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 14: can unchanged coverage be reported as preserved behavior? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-682` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 15: can a coding agent bypass required analysis, ignore a blocking Analyzer Finding Record, or self-approve an exception? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-683` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 16: can untrusted repository content cause command execution, path escape, network access, secret exposure, or analyzer compromise? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-684` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 17: can a concrete provider or storage choice reshape the semantic model for its convenience? |
| `CSAA-000-REQ-685` | Each authored document SHALL be reviewed against failure mode 18: can the prose remain “implemented” while no enforced artifact performs its requirements? |

#### 3.2.1 Controlled-polarity reconciliation

The direct-canon rows retain their source-controlled affirmative/prohibitive force. Each §3.2 row appears exactly once below:

| Source group | Affirmative mandatory row IDs | Prohibitive mandatory row IDs | Reconciliation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `JPWB-CON-000` | `CON-002`, `003`, `004`, `005`, `010`, `011` | `CON-001`, `006`, `007`, `008`, `009`, `012`, `013`, `014`, `015` | 6 + 9 = 15 |
| `JPWB-DOC-001` | `D001-003`, `009`, `010`, `011` | `D001-001`, `002`, `004`, `005`, `006`, `007`, `008` | 4 + 7 = 11 |
| `JPWB-DOC-002` | `D002-002`, `003`, `007` | `D002-001`, `004`, `005`, `006` | 3 + 4 = 7 |
| `JPWB-DOC-003` | `D003-001`, `003`, `006`, `010`, `011`, `013`, `015`, `017`, `019`, `020` | `D003-002`, `004`, `005`, `007`, `008`, `009`, `012`, `014`, `016`, `018` | 10 + 10 = 20 |
| `JPWB-DOC-004` | `D004-001`, `002`, `004`, `005`, `006`, `007`, `009`, `010`, `011`, `012`, `015`, `017` | `D004-003`, `008`, `013`, `014`, `016`, `018` | 12 + 6 = 18 |
| **Total** | **35** | **36** | **71** |

Every shorthand above expands under the `CSAA-005-INH-` prefix. Polarity classifies the controlled obligation's governing force; it does not replace the atomic obligation wording or its exact source.

### Allowed obligation-disposition values

- `APPLICABLE_NOW`
- `ALLOCATED_TO_ACTIVE_DOCUMENTATION_SUBPHASE`
- `ALLOCATED_TO_POST_LEDGER_SELF_REVIEW`
- `ALLOCATED_TO_LATER_EXECUTION`
- `ALLOCATED_TO_LATER_LIFECYCLE`
- `ALLOCATED_TO_LATER_WAVE`
- `CONDITION_SATISFIED_SUCCESSOR_MODEL_CONTROLS`
- `DEFERRED_BY_CITED_AUTHORITY`
- `WAIVED_BY_CITED_AUTHORITY`
- `DIVERGENT_WITH_FINDING`
- `NOT_APPLICABLE_WITH_RATIONALE`

### Allowed implementation-state values

- `NOT_STARTED`
- `PLANNED`
- `IMPLEMENTED`
- `NOT_REQUIRED_CURRENT_PHASE`

### Allowed row verification-state values

- `NOT_RUN`
- `PASSED`
- `FAILED`
- `BLOCKED`
- `INCONCLUSIVE`
- `STALE`
- `UNSUPPORTED`
- `NOT_REQUIRED_CURRENT_PHASE`

`ALLOCATED_TO_POST_LEDGER_SELF_REVIEW` means the obligation is applicable to this member but can be performed only after objective Draft-closure verification has closed the exact ledger. `ALLOCATED_TO_LATER_LIFECYCLE` means the obligation belongs to the same member's ordered lifecycle but cannot be performed in the current Draft phase. Each allocation names its exact activation gate and uses `NOT_REQUIRED_CURRENT_PHASE` for implementation and verification without claiming satisfaction or pass.

Disposition, implementation, and verification are independent. All 458 applicable obligations pass only under direct-current VERIFICATION-003: exactly 387 state-bearing raw rows transition to `PASSED`, while 71 byte-stable direct-canon rows are verified through shared current methods. All 37 noncurrent rows remain explicit nonpasses. Objective closure does not perform SELF, final refresh, cross-package reconciliation, or later lifecycle.

---

## 4. Requirement-row rules

Every row retains stable identity, owner, version/section, non-weakened obligation, exact applicability, disposition authority, site, separate implementation and verification states, verification binding, oracle owner, and evidence.

Independent-obligation compression is prohibited. Every allocation, deferral, waiver, divergence, or not-applicability disposition includes competent authority.

Corrective objective closure evaluates all 458 applicable obligations through direct-current author/ledger-closer verification: 387 raw row-state transitions plus shared-method verification for 71 byte-stable direct-canon rows. The 18 SELF rows remain nonpasses and cannot complete until the final implementation refresh and affected reruns. Every later-lifecycle, execution, successor-model, not-applicable, and deferred row retains its exact noncurrent disposition.

---
